\boldsymbol{x}
for
\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})
in a polynomial of
\Psi

, and so

has an inverse

a_{h}^{-1}
modulo

. The system of non-congruences

\mathcal{S}_{k+1}
given by

\displaystyle y_{k+1}

\displaystyle\not\equiv-a_{h}^{-1}\cdot c_{h}

\displaystyle\pmod{p}

\displaystyle h\in\textup{terms}(\Psi^{\prime})\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(h)=y_{k+1}.

From Condition
(P1)
we have
p>\#\textup{terms}(\Psi)\geq\#\textup{terms}(\Psi^{\prime})
, and so

to be an element in

that differs from every
-a_{h}^{-1}\cdot c_{h}

appearing in the rows of
\mathcal{S}_{k+1}
.

The solution
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
resulting from the systems of

\mathcal{S}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{S}_{j}
is such that,
for every

.
Therefore,
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
is a solution for
\Psi^{\prime}
modulo

p
.

case
p\mathrel{|}\alpha_{i}
for some
i\in[\ell+1,n]
.

This case is involved. Since
p
divides
some
\alpha_{i}=f_{i}(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}))
, and
p\not\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, by Condition

, where

by

.
Recall that
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})
with

,

. Below, let us define

\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
for
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{j})
. We rely on the following induction

IH1
′
:

for every non-zero polynomial

.

Let us first show that by constructing

modulo

no matter what is the

.

Note moreover that (
IH1′:
)
and (

) directly imply
\max\{v_{p}(g(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))\in\mathbb{N}:g\in\textup{terms}(\Psi^{\prime})\}\leq u
.

To conclude the proof, we show how to construct
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}

satisfying (
IH1′:
)
and (
IH2′:
).

base case
k=0
.

We establish (
IH1′:
)
and (
IH2′:
) for polynomials

\boldsymbol{x}
, by showing the three

with
{\textup{LV}(h)\preceq x_{d}}
.

1.

{\mathbb{Z}f\cap\mathbb{Z}h\neq\{0\}}

.

These three items imply (

)
and (

). To

), take

).

Proof of
(
1
): Since
\Psi
has the
elimination property,
f\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)
.
Then, (
1
) follows directly
from (
IH2:
); remark that

S(f,h)=0
is equivalent to
\mathbb{Z}f\cap\mathbb{Z}h\neq\{0\}
.

Proof of
(
2
): By
\mathbb{Z}f\cap\mathbb{Z}h\neq\{0\}
there are
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
such that
\lambda_{1}\cdot f=\lambda_{2}\cdot h
.
Without loss of generality,
\gcd(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})=1
, and thus
\gcd(\lambda_{2},\gcd(f))=\lambda_{2}
. The
polynomial
f
is primitive, hence
\lambda_{2}=1
and we
get
h=\lambda_{1}\cdot f
. Since
p\not\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, from Condition
(P2)
and

we derive

.
Therefore,
v_{p}(h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})))=v_{p}(\lambda_{1}\cdot f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})))=v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})))
.

Proof of
(
3
): Trivially,
p\nmid h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}))
equals
v_{p}(h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})))=0
. To
show
{\mathbb{Z}h\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)=\{0\}}
, first note
that
\mathbb{Z}h\cap\mathbb{Z}f=\{0\}
, directly from
p\mathrel{|}f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}))
and (
2
).
Ad
absurdum
, assume
\mathbb{Z}h\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\not=\{0\}
. Since
\Psi
is increasing for
\boldsymbol{\chi}\coloneqq(X_{1}\mathbin{\prec}\dots\mathbin{\prec}X_{r})
, and
\textup{LV}(h)

and
\textup{LV}(f)
are both in

,
\Psi
is increasing no

. Take
an order

for which

, and let
{x_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\prec}^{\prime}\dots\mathbin{\prec}^{\prime}x_{d}^{\prime}}
be the

x_{1},\dots,x_{d}
. Since
\Psi
is

\mathbin{\prec}^{\prime}
,
\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\cap\mathbb{Z}[x_{1}^{\prime},\dots,x_{\textup{LV}_{\mathbin{\prec}^{\prime}}(f)}^{\prime}]=\mathbb{Z}f
. However,

by definition of
\mathbin{\prec}^{\prime}

).

induction step.

Let us assume that

b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k}

, so that the

)
and (

) satisfied. We divide

such

\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
.

case

In this case, (

) is

, so we

). It suffices

\displaystyle h(b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k},\,y_{k+1})

\displaystyle\not\equiv 0

\displaystyle\pmod{p}

\displaystyle h\in\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(h)=y_{k+1}.

Similarly to the system in
Equation

13
,
writing
c_{h}+a_{h}\cdot y_{k+1}
for

h(b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k},\,y_{k+1})
, we obtain the
equivalent system of non-congruences

\displaystyle y_{k+1}

\displaystyle\not\equiv-a_{h}^{-1}\cdot c_{h}

\displaystyle\pmod{p}

\displaystyle h\in\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(h)=y_{k+1}.

Since
p\not\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
and
from
(P1)
, this system admits a solution

in
[0,p-1]
. Note

) is satisfied,

case
g
exists.

Recall that
g
is a polynomial
in
\textup{terms}(\Psi)
such that
\textup{LV}(g)=y_{k+1}
and
\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
. Let
u\coloneqq v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})))
. In order to
satisfy (
IH1′:
) it suffices to
find
b_{p,k+1}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying the following
(non-empty) system of non-congruences

\displaystyle\forall g\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(g)=y_{k+1}\text{ and }\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\},

\displaystyle\qquad\begin{aligned} g(b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k},\,y_{k+1})&\equiv 0&&\pmod{p^{u}}\\
g(b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k},\,y_{k+1})&\not\equiv 0&&\pmod{p^{u+1}}.\end{aligned}

Similarly to the system in
Equation

13
,
writing
c_{g}+a_{g}\cdot y_{k+1}
for

g(b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k},\,y_{k+1})
, we obtain the
equivalent system of non-congruences

\displaystyle\forall g\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(g)=y_{k+1}\text{ and }\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\},

(14)

\displaystyle\qquad\begin{aligned} y_{k+1}&\equiv-a_{g}^{-1}\cdot c_{g}&&\pmod{p^{u}}\\
y_{k+1}&\not\equiv-a_{g}^{-1}\cdot c_{g}&&\pmod{p^{u+1}}.\end{aligned}

Focus on the congruences
y_{k+1}\equiv-a_{g}^{-1}\cdot c_{g}\pmod{p^{u}}
of this system. These only have a
solution if the right-hand side is the same modulo
p^{u}

for every
g\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)
with
\textup{LV}(g)=y_{k+1}

and
\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
. We prove
that this is indeed the case. Consider
g_{1}
and
g_{2}

such that
g_{i}\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)
with
\textup{LV}(g_{i})=y_{k+1}
and
{\mathbb{Z}g_{i}\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}}
, for
{i\in\{1,2\}}
. Let
\lambda_{1}
and

\lambda_{2}
be the smallest positive integers such that
both
\lambda_{1}\cdot g_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}\cdot g_{2}

belong to
\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)
. By definition of
divisibility module and

-polynomial
,

{S(\lambda_{1}\cdot g_{1},\lambda_{2}\cdot g_{2})\in\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\cap\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\dots,x_{d},y_{1},\dots,y_{k}]}
.
According to the elimination property of
\Psi
, there
is a (finite) basis
B
for
\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\cap\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\dots,x_{d},y_{1},\dots,y_{k}]
such that for every

{h\in B}
,
f\mathrel{|}h
is a divisibility in
\Psi
.
Moreover,
\textup{LV}(h)\preceq y_{k}
and thus
by (
IH1′:
) we get
v_{p}(h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}),b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k}))=u
. Now, since

S(\lambda_{1}\cdot g_{1},\lambda_{2}\cdot g_{2})
is a
linear combination of elements in
B
, we conclude that

p^{u}\mathrel{|}S(\lambda_{1}\cdot g_{1},\lambda_{2}\cdot g_{2})
.
By writing
g_{i}(\boldsymbol{x},y_{1},\dots,y_{k+1})
as

g_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},y_{1},\dots,y_{k})+a_{i}\cdot y_{k+1}
, for

i\in\{1,2\}
, this divisibility can be rewritten as
the congruence:

(\lambda_{2}\cdot a_{2})\cdot(\lambda_{1}\cdot g_{1}^{\prime})\equiv(\lambda_{1}\cdot a_{1})\cdot(\lambda_{2}\cdot g_{2}^{\prime})\pmod{p^{u}}.

From
p\not\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
,
(P2)

and
(P3)
, we conclude that
p\nmid\lambda_{1}\cdot\lambda_{2}\cdot a_{1}\cdot a_{2}
. By multiplying
both sides of the above congruence by the inverse

(\lambda_{1}\cdot\lambda_{2}\cdot a_{1}\cdot a_{2})^{-1}

of
\lambda_{1}\cdot\lambda_{2}\cdot a_{1}\cdot a_{2}

modulo
p^{u}
, we conclude that
a_{1}^{-1}\cdot g_{1}^{\prime}\equiv a_{2}^{-1}\cdot g_{2}^{\prime}\pmod{p^{u}}
. This shows that
the right-hand side is the same across all the
congruences and non-congruences of the system
in
Equation

14
. Moreover,
p>\#\textup{terms}(\Psi)
by
(P1)
, and therefore
this system is feasible, and more precisely has a
solution
b_{p,k+1}
of the form
b_{p,k+1}\coloneqq p^{u}\cdot\gamma
for some
\gamma\in[1,p-1]
. Pick
such a solution, which by construction
satisfies (
IH1′:
).

We show that
b_{p,k+1}
also
satisfies (
IH2′:
). Here is where
the existence of the polynomial
{g\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)}

satisfying
\textup{LV}(g)=y_{k+1}
and
\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
plays a role. From
\mathbb{Z}g\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
and since
\Psi
has
the elimination property, we can find a polynomial
g_{0}

such that
f\mathrel{|}g_{0}
is in
\Psi
, and
\textup{LV}(g_{0})=y_{k+1}
. We prove (
IH2′:
)
arguing by contraposition. Let
h\in\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)

such that
\textup{LV}(h)=y_{k+1}
and
p\mathrel{|}h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}),b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k+1})
. If
S(h,g_{0})
is zero,
i.e.,
h
and
g_{0}
are linearly dependent, then
\mathbb{Z}h\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
follows by definition
of
g_{0}
, and (
IH2′:
) holds for

h
. Suppose that
S(h,g_{0})
is non-zero. From the
construction of
b_{p,k+1}
and since
g_{0}
is a
polynomial considered in
Equation

14
,
we have
p\mathrel{|}g_{0}(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}),b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k+1})
. Then, by definition of

S
-polynomial,
p\mathrel{|}S(h,g_{0})(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}),b_{p,1},\dots,b_{p,k})
. By definition of

\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)
, note that
h\in\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)

and
g_{0}\in\textup{terms}(\Psi)
implies
S(h,g_{0})\in\mathrm{S}_{f}(\Psi)
. Since
S(h,g_{0})
is non-zero, the
induction hypothesis (
IH2′:
)
implies that
\mathbb{Z}S(h,g_{0})\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}
. Then,
\mathbb{Z}h\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)\neq\{0\}

follows directly from the fact that
f\mathrel{|}g_{0}
appears
in
\Psi
(and so
\mathbb{Z}g_{0}\cap\textup{M}_{f}(\Psi)
). Once
more, we conclude that (
IH2′:
)
holds for
h
.

Following the case analysis above, we construct solutions

\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
for
\Psi^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{y})
modulo
p
, for every
p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi^{\prime})
. This concludes the proof
of
5
.
∎

Appendix F
Theorem

4
: proof
of
8

We recall that
\underline{O}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is the minimal
positive integer greater or equal than
4
such that the map

{x\mapsto\underline{O}(x+1)}
upper bounds the linear
functions hidden in the
O(.)
appearing
in
Lemma

7
.
The integer
\Gamma(r,\ell,w,m,d)
, with
r,\ell,w,m,d\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
r\leq d
,
is the maximum bit length of the minimal positive solution
of any system of divisibility constraints
\Phi
such that:

•

\Phi
is
r
-increasing.

•

The maximum bit length of a coefficient or constant
appearing in
\Phi
, i.e.,
\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle
, is at most

\ell
.

•

For every
p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)
, consider a solution

\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
of
\Phi
modulo
p
minimizing
\mu_{p}\coloneqq\max\{{v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))}:f\text{ is in the
left-hand side of a divisibility in }\Phi\}
. Then,

\log_{2}\left(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\right)\leq w
.

•

\Phi
has at most
m
divisibilities.

•

\Phi
has at most
d
variables.

Since we want to find an upper bound for
\Gamma
, assume
without loss of generality that
\Gamma(r,\ell,w,m,d)
is
always at least
\min(\ell,w)
. Let us prove
8
.

See
8

Analysis on
\Gamma(1,\ell,w,m,d)
:

This case corresponds to the base case of the main
induction, where the solutions are found thanks to the
system of congruences in
Equation

4
, where
for
p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)
,
\mu_{p}\coloneqq\max\{v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p})):f\text{ is in the left-hand side of a
divisibility of }\Phi\}
. From the Chinese remainder
theorem, this system of congruences has a solution where
every variable is in
[1,\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}]
. Therefore, every variable is bounded by

2^{w}
by definition of
w
, and therefore its bit length
is bounded by
w+3
, since
\langle x\rangle=1+\left\lceil\log_{2}(|x|+1)\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\log_{2}(|x|)\right\rceil+2\leq\log_{2}(|x|)+3
, and
w
is positive.

Analysis on
\Gamma(r,\ell,w,m,d)
with
r\geq 2
:

This case corresponds to the induction step of the main
induction, where the solutions are found thanks to the
system of (non)congruences in
Equation

6
.
At the start of the induction, we add the elimination
property to
\Phi
. According
to
Lemma

7
, we obtain a system

\Psi
with
n\leq m\cdot(d+2)
divisibilities and

\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Psi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle\leq\underline{O}(m^{3}d+1)\cdot\log_{2}((d+1)(m+{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}+2))+3
. We find solutions
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
for
\Psi
modulo
p
,
for every
p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
. For
p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)
,
these are the solutions
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
for
\Phi
modulo
p

stated in the hypothesis of the theorem. For
p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\setminus\mathbb{P}(\Phi)
, we compute
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}

as a solution for
\Phi
modulo
p
, taken such that for
every
f
left-hand side of a divisibility in
\Phi
,

v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))=0
. The existence of such a solution is
guaranteed by
Lemma

3
, and as discussed
when presenting the procedure the vector
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
is a
solution for
\Psi
modulo
p
such that for every
f

left-hand side of a divisibility in
\Psi
,
v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))=0
. As usual, given
p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, let
\mu_{p}\coloneqq\max\{v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p})):f\text{ is in the
left-hand side of a divisibility of }\Psi\}
.

Suppose that the set
X_{1}=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{d^{\prime}}\}
of
variables considered in this step is ordered as
{x_{1}\mathbin{\prec}\dots\mathbin{\prec}x_{d^{\prime}}}
(with
d^{\prime}\leq d
). Recall
that the values assigned to these variables are chosen
inductively, starting with
x_{1}
and following the
order
\mathbin{\prec}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\nu}
be the map computed in this
way. Given
k\in[0,d-1]
, at the
(k+1)
-th iteration we
defined the set
P_{k}
as

P_{k}\coloneqq\left\{p\in\mathbb{P}:p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\text{ or there is }h\in S(\Delta(\Psi))\backslash\{0\}\text{ s.t.}~\textup{LV}(h)\preceq x_{k}\text{ and }p\mid h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}))\right\},

and added to it the smallest prime not in
\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, if
the above definition yields
P_{k}=\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
.

For simplicity, below let
s\coloneqq\#S(\Delta(\Psi))
,
t\coloneqq{|\mkern-2.0mu|}S(\Delta(\Psi)){|\mkern-2.0mu|}
and
w_{1}\coloneqq\log_{2}(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1})
, which are all
at least
1
.

Inductively on
k\in[0,d-1]
, we show that

\log_{2}(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{k+1}))\leq B
where

B\coloneqq C\cdot(\log_{2}(C))^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad C\coloneqq 2^{4}\cdot w_{1}\cdot s^{3}\cdot\big(5+\log_{2}\log_{2}(t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{2}.

Therefore,
\langle\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{k+1})\rangle\leq B+3\leq 2^{18}\cdot s^{4}\cdot\big(5+\log_{2}\log_{2}(t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{3}\cdot w_{1}\cdot(\log_{2}(w_{1})+2)^{3}
,
where this last inequality follows from a straightforward
computation together with the fact that
(\log_{2}(x))^{3}\leq 5\cdot x
for every
x\geq 1
. Note that we do not
simplify
(\log_{2}(w_{1}+2))^{3}
into
5\cdot(w_{1}+2)
,
as this would yield an exponentially worse bound for

\Gamma(r,\ell,\eta,m,d)
later on.

base case
k=0
.

In this case,
P_{0}=\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\cup\{p\}
where
p

is the smallest prime not in
\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
. Then,

\#P_{0}=\#\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)+1
. We bound
\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
by applying
Theorem

3
to
the system of (non)congruences
in
Equation

6
. We get:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Big)\cdot\big((s+1)\cdot\#(P_{0}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))\big)^{4\cdot(s+1)^{2}(3+\ln\ln(\#(P_{0}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))+1))}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Big)\cdot(s+1)^{12\cdot(s+1)^{2}}

Therefore,
\log_{2}(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1}))\leq w_{1}+12\cdot(s+1)^{2}\log(s+1)
.

induction step
k\geq 1
.

Let us first bound
\#(P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))
. By definition,

P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)=\{p\in\mathbb{P}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi):\textup{LV}(h)\preceq x_{k}\text{ and }p\mathrel{|}h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}))\text{ for some }h\in S(\Delta(\Psi))\setminus\{0\}\}.

By induction hypothesis, for every
h\in S(\Delta(\Psi))
,
|h(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}))|\leq(k\cdot 2^{B}+1)\cdot t
, and therefore

\#(P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))\leq s\cdot\log_{2}((k\cdot 2^{B}+1)\cdot t)\leq s\cdot\log_{2}(2^{B}\cdot t\cdot(d+1))
. Note that
s\cdot\log_{2}(2^{B}\cdot t\cdot(d+1))\geq 1
, hence this
bound on
\#(P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))
already
capture the case where one prime had to be added to

P_{k}
in order to make this set different form

\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
. We bound
\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
by
applying
Theorem

3
to the system of
(non)congruences in
Equation

6
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Big)\cdot\big((s+1)\cdot\#(P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))\big)^{4\cdot(s+1)^{2}(3+\ln\ln(\#(P_{k}\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi))+1))}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Big)\cdot\big((s+1)^{2}\cdot\log_{2}(2^{B}t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{4\cdot(s+1)^{2}(3+\ln\ln(1+s\cdot\log_{2}(2^{B}t\cdot(d+1))))}.

Then, a simple analysis using properties of logarithms
shows that

\log_{2}(\boldsymbol{\nu}(x_{k+1}))
is at most

\displaystyle 2^{4}\cdot w_{1}\cdot s^{3}\cdot\big(5+\log_{2}\log_{2}(t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{2}\cdot(\log_{2}(B))^{2}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle C\cdot(\log_{2}(B))^{2}

definition of
C
.

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle B,

where the latter inequality holds from the fact that,
whenever
C\geq 45
, every element
x_{i}
of the
recurrence relation
{\big(x_{0}=C,\ x_{i+1}=C\cdot(\log_{2}(x_{i}))^{2}\big)}
is bounded by
C\cdot(\log_{2}(C))^{3}
, i.e.,
B
.

We have established that the bit length of the solutions for
the variables in
X_{1}
can be bounded with
B+3
. Next, we
want to bound
B+3
using the arguments of
\Gamma
. To do
so, we first derive upper bounds for
s
,
t
and
w_{1}
.
For
s
and
t
, from
Lemma

9
we obtain
s\leq 8\cdot m^{4}\cdot(d+2)^{6}
and
\log_{2}(t)\leq 2\cdot(d+2)\cdot(\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+1)+1
. For
w_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\setminus\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\cdot\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\setminus\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)+w

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)\setminus\mathbb{P}(\Phi)}p\Bigr)+w

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\mu_{p}=0\text{ for all }p\not\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p\Bigr)+w

\displaystyle\leq 64\cdot n^{5}(d+2)^{4}(\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Psi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+2)+w

by
Lemma

4

\displaystyle\leq 64\cdot(m\cdot(d+2))^{5}(d+2)^{4}(\underline{O}(m^{3}d+1)\cdot\log_{2}((d+1)(m+{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}+2))+5)+w

\displaystyle\leq 128\cdot\underline{O}\cdot m^{9}(d+2)^{11}\cdot(\ell+w).

Then,
B+3
is bounded as follows:

\displaystyle B+3

\displaystyle\leq 2^{18}\cdot s^{4}\cdot\big(5+\log_{2}\log_{2}(t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{3}\cdot w_{1}\cdot(\log_{2}(w_{1})+2)^{3}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{30}\cdot m^{16}(d+2)^{24}\big(5+\log_{2}\log_{2}(t\cdot(d+1))\big)^{3}\cdot w_{1}\cdot(\log_{2}(w_{1})+2)^{3}

\displaystyle\text{bound on }s

\displaystyle\leq 2^{38}\cdot m^{16}(d+2)^{25}(1+\log_{2}(\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Psi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+1))^{3}\cdot w_{1}\cdot(\log_{2}(w_{1})+2)^{3}

bound on
\log_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{54}\cdot m^{17}(d+2)^{26}\log_{2}(\underline{O})^{3}\cdot(2+\log_{2}(\ell))^{3}\cdot w_{1}\cdot(\log_{2}(w_{1})+2)^{3}

bound on
\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Psi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{104}\cdot m^{27}(d+2)^{38}\underline{O}\cdot\log_{2}(\underline{O})^{6}\cdot(\ell+w)\cdot(\log_{2}(\ell+w))^{6}

\displaystyle\text{bound on $w_{1}$}.

The procedure continues by recursively computing a positive
integer solution for the formula
\Phi^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{y})\coloneqq\Phi[\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})\mathbin{/}x:x\in X_{1}]
, which is

s
-increasing for some
s\leq r-1
. In the recursion, the
procedure uses solutions
\boldsymbol{b}_{p}
for
\Phi^{\prime}
modulo
p

for every
p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})
, computed according
to
7
. Hence, to
conclude the analysis on
\Gamma
, it suffices to find
positive integers
\ell^{\prime},w^{\prime},m^{\prime},d^{\prime}
such that
\Phi^{\prime}
is
one of the formulae considered for
\Gamma(r-1,\ell^{\prime},w^{\prime},m^{\prime},d^{\prime})
. Let us bound these integers:

•

\Phi^{\prime}
has fewer variables and divisibilities than

\Phi
, therefore we can choose
m^{\prime}=m
and
d^{\prime}=d
.

•

The coefficients of the variables in the polynomials
of
\Phi^{\prime}
are all from
\Phi
, therefore their bit-length
is bounded by
\ell
. Let us bound the constants of the
polynomials in
\Phi^{\prime}
. These constants have the form

f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x}))
with
f
being a polynomial with
coefficients and constant bounded from
\Phi
. So,

\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}f(\boldsymbol{\nu}(\boldsymbol{x})){|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle\leq\langle 2^{B}\cdot{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\cdot d+{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle
, and from the
bounds on
B+3
we can set

\displaystyle\ell^{\prime}=2^{105}\cdot m^{27}(d+2)^{38}\underline{O}\cdot\log_{2}(\underline{O})^{6}\cdot(\ell+w)\cdot(\log_{2}(\ell+w))^{6}.

•

Let
\mu_{p}\coloneqq\max\{{v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))}:f\text{ is in the left-hand side of a divisibility in }\Phi^{\prime}\}
. Thanks
to
7
, if
p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, then
\mu_{p}=\max\{{v_{p}(f(\boldsymbol{b}_{p}))}:f\text{ is in the left-hand side of a divisibility in }\Psi\}
, and otherwise if
p\not\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)
, then

\mu_{p}
is the
p
-adic valuation of a constant left-hand
side of
\Phi^{\prime}
. We derive the following bound
on
\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)
, which yields a value for
w^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)+\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})\cap\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}}\Bigr)+\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})\setminus\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p\Bigr)+\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbf{P}_{\!\!+}(\Psi)}p^{\mu_{p}+1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\text{ constant and}\\
\text{left-hand side~in $\Phi^{\prime}$}\end{subarray}}\alpha\Bigr)+\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})}p\Bigr)+w_{1}

from
7

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle m\cdot\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi^{\prime}{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+\log_{2}\Bigl(\prod_{p\in\mathbb{P}(\Phi^{\prime})}p\Bigr)+w_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle m\cdot\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi^{\prime}{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+m^{2}(d+2)(\langle{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\Phi^{\prime}{|\mkern-2.0mu|}\rangle+2)+w_{1}

from
Lemma

4

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2^{109}\cdot m^{29}(d+2)^{39}\underline{O}\cdot\log_{2}(\underline{O})^{6}\cdot(\ell+w)\cdot(\log_{2}(\ell+w))^{6}=w^{\prime}.

Note that since the bound we obtained for
\ell^{\prime}
is greater
than
B+3
, the value

\Gamma(r-1,\ 2^{104}\cdot m^{27}(d+2)^{38}\underline{O}\cdot\log_{2}(\underline{O})^{6}\cdot(\ell+w)\cdot(\log_{2}(\ell+w))^{6},\ w^{\prime},\ m,\ d)

bounds not
only the bit length of the minimal positive solution of

\Phi^{\prime}
, but also of the solutions assigned to variables
in
X_{1}
. This concludes the proof of
8
.
